CursorHope of Acklam · 25%
Risk Unproven at 8f against higher-OR rivals who may outstay him.
Hope of Acklam official ratingpace map / early speed8f stamina vs his Redcar 7f form
**Winner case:** Hope of Acklam is the most convincing angle in a wide-open Class 6. He finished second at Redcar on Good ground (7f, 0.3L, 11 days ago) — the clearest course-and-going evidence in the field — and followed with a respectable third at Musselburgh on a big price, suggesting he is running to a mark rather than fluking a track bias. Draw 8 sits in the high third where Redcar 8f handicaps (7–10 runners) have won most often (38%), and he is fresh (11 days) with Billy Garritty retained from the Redcar near-miss. At 4.3 he is co-favourite, but that price reflects the form rather than defining it; I am not simply endorsing the market leader without cause.

**Biggest risk:** He is stepping up to a mile for the first time while several rivals (Son of A Fish, Dakota Brave) bring higher exposed ORs and recent seven-furlong form that may translate better if this stays strongly run. The Easterby yard also runs Diplomacy, which adds tactical uncertainty, and his own OR is unlisted — if he is harshly treated on the back of the Redcar run, the progressive Hannon horse or a well-drawn Astral Calling (stall 10) could pass him in the final two furlongs.

Dakota Brave shows consistent form at the track and distance on good ground, but the 8f trip on good to firm is a step up from his recent 7f efforts, and his draw of 3 in a 10-runner field is moderate rather than ideal given Redcar's bias toward low draws. Boiling Point is my pick chiefly on course-and-distance proof: he won this exact Goodwood 10-mile Class 1 on good ground last May (analog winner), returns with the top OR (112), and maps to the high draw that has dominated small-field 10f races here (52% winners). His July second over nine furlongs confirms current form, and Naqeeb’s narrow defeat behind him in that May renewal validates the track rather than flattery. I am not deferring to Lion’s Pride or Quisana — the former is 145 days off with poor recent figures and no good-ground wins, the latter is 35lb below on ratings with only handicap-class form.

The main risk is that Respond brings the card’s strongest trainer-jockey combination (Balding/Murphy) with respectable 10f good-ground form and enough class to outrun his 105 OR if the expected pace-on scenario (Boiling Point, Treble Tee) sets it up, or if Burke/Sam James’s cold recent strike rates blunt the favourite’s edge. Naqeeb is the clear second: same race 0.1L behind Boiling Point last year and course form figures of 1-3 and 1-3 at CD, though draw one is the wrong third of the gate here.

Hidden Fortune is the right default: two hurdle wins on good ground (Bangor and Fontwell), fit off 15 days, and the Dan/Harry Skelton pairing is the strongest card stat here (both top-five nationally, 5–24 / 5–22 in the last fortnight). The 11 form is a clean jumping profile with no P/F/U, and he maps as the most reliable improver in a field littered with pull-ups, falls, and long absences. Letterston Lily and Dolly Dior are the main alternatives — Lily off a win with CD 1–1 and Bowen in form, Dolly Dior progressive (4121) with a Hexham 20f win and Perth course form — but both carry more patchy earlier form and neither matches the Skelton completion-and-fitness package at the head of the market.

The biggest risk is trip: Hidden Fortune has not raced at Perth or beyond ~17f110y over hurdles, and this 20f handicap asks a stamina question the post-mortem lessons flag when yards target shorter wins first. A progressive, lightly raced handicapper can still win stepping up, but it is the one reason not to treat the short price as proof rather than a fair reflection of connections and ground form.

This is a messy nine-runner Class 4 handicap where the market favourite looks misguided on trip. **Wardlaw** is 3.2 from a hood-assisted Beverley sprint win and a Thirsk fourth over four furlongs — nothing in the profile shouts eleven furlongs on AW, and draw three sits in the weakest third at this track (low stalls 27% vs high 41%). **What's The Plan** has the clearest stamina evidence on the page — back-to-back seconds at Sandown and Lingfield over ten furlongs — but stall one is a material negative here, the surface is untested, and Ashley Lewis claims three; I would not treat 4.8 as proof.

**Joseph** is the reasoned play at 8.6: Billy Loughnane is the card’s top jockey, draw six is neutral-to-favourable, he has won on this AW surface and was only 0.3L off at Wolverhampton over twelve furlongs — the best AW staying signal in the field from a horse that is 7lb better off at the weights than the favourite. The main reservation is that his recent Kempton success came over three furlongs, so the step to eleven is still a query even if the trip map and contested pace (Qitaal/Narragansett forward) should suit a prominent type.

CursorEabha · 20%
Risk Coral Cove’s superior recent 7f figures could outweigh Eabha if draw bias is weaker than history suggests in this 16-runner setup.
official ratings for unrated runnerspace map / early speed distributionwhich Charlie Fellowes and K.R. Burke runners are favoured
Eabha looks the most solid win angle in a wide-open 16-runner Class 2. She brings the clearest 7f-on-good proof — an authoritative Epsom win and a Newmarket (July) success — with an OR 81 that fits this grade without relying on market warmth. Draw 10 is neutral in a field where low stalls have only a modest edge (~46% winners), and she avoids the worst of the draw map unlike Coral Cove (16) or Fillipas (15). Zigazig Ah tops the ratings on OR 85 but his profile is sprint-heavy (5f–6f) with a modest Goodwood course run already; I’m not treating that mark as decisive at 7f here.

The main risk is that this is still a deep, partially unmapped heat: Coral Cove’s recent 7f form (York, Doncaster, Ripon) is arguably hotter on figures and only loses significant ground on draw, while Kodi Bear Light (dr5, G 1-2) and China In Your Hand (7f winner, better draw than Coral Cove) are live if Fellowes/Burke have their priorities right. Wise And Wonderful’s 0.2L defeat in this exact CD last time keeps him in the each-way picture despite top weight, but I prefer Eabha’s cleaner trip profile and winning rhythm.
